. Applying the long exact sequence in compactly-supported cohomology for the open-closed decomposition
\overline{M}_{\mathcal{C}}=M_{\mathcal{C}}\bigsqcup U
we find that we have

H_{c}(M_{\mathcal{C}},q,t)=q^{2}t^{4}+3qt^{2}+t^{2}.

(8.1.6)

From eq.(
8.1.6
), using the long exact sequence for the open-closed decomposition
M_{\mathcal{C}}=(M_{\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{m\})\bigsqcup\{m\}
we deduce that we have:

H_{c}(\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}},q,t)=H_{c}(M_{\mathcal{C}}\setminus\{m\},q,t)=q^{2}t^{4}+3qt^{2}+t^{2}+t.

(8.1.7)

8.1.2
Cohomology of the character stack in the non-generic case

We introduce the following notations. Let
Y_{\mathcal{C}}=X_{\mathcal{C}}\setminus O
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}=[Y_{\mathcal{C}}/\PGl_{2}]
. The action of
\PGl_{2}
on
Y_{\mathcal{C}}
is set-theoretically free so that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}
is at least an algebraic space.

Notice that there is an isomorphism
[O/\PGl_{2}]\cong B\mathbb{G}_{m}
and an open-closed decomposition

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}}=\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}\bigsqcup[O/\PGl_{2}].

Applying the long-exact sequence for compactly supported cohomology for the open-closed decomposition above and knowing that
H^{*}_{c}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
is concentrated in strictly negative even degrees, we obtain

H_{c}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{C}},q,t)=H_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}},q,t)+H_{c}(B\mathbb{G}_{m},q,t)=H_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}},q,t)+\dfrac{1}{qt^{2}-1}.

(8.1.8)

We have thus reduced ourselves to compute the mixed Poincaré series
H_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}},q,t)
. Let
Y_{\mathcal{C}}^{+}=Y_{\mathcal{C}}\setminus Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{-}
and
Y_{\mathcal{C}}^{-}=Y_{\mathcal{C}}\setminus Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{+}
. Notice that, a priori,
Y_{\mathcal{C}}^{+},Y^{-}_{\mathcal{C}}
are only constructible subsets of
X_{\mathcal{C}}
. Therefore we don’t have a good definition of the quotient stacks
[Y_{\mathcal{C}}^{+}/\PGl_{2}],[Y_{\mathcal{C}}^{-}/\PGl_{2}]
neither of their cohomology.

To solve this problem, we start by the following preliminary Lemma.

Lemma 8.1.1
.

Let
G
be a linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{C}
acting on the left on a
\mathbb{C}
-scheme
X
. Let
H\leq G
be a closed subgroup. Suppose that there exists a
G
-equivariant map
p:X\to G/H
, where
G
acts on
G/H
by left multiplication. Denote by
X_{H}=p^{-1}(eH)
.

The group
H
acts on
X_{H}
and there is an isomorphism of quotient stacks

[X/G]\cong[X_{H}/H].

(8.1.9)

Moreover, if
X
is an affine variety and
G,H
are reductive, there is an isomorphism of varieties:

X/\!/G\cong X_{H}/\!/H.

(8.1.10)

Proof.

Notice firstly that, if
X
is affine and
G,H
are reductive, the isomorphism (
8.1.10
) is implied by the isomorphism (
8.1.9
) as the varieties
X_{H}/\!/H,X/\!/G
are good moduli spaces for the stacks
[X_{H}/H],[X/G]
respectively (see
[
3
, Remark 4.8]
).
We now prove isomorphism (
8.1.9
).

Notice that in general there is always a map
\alpha:[X_{H}/H]\to[X/G]
. We must construct an inverse
\beta:[X_{H}/H]\to[X/G]
.

Fix a scheme
S
and recall that the objects of the groupoid
[X/G](S)
are couples
(P,\varphi)
where
P\to S
is a principal
G
-bundle and
\varphi:P\to X
is a
G
-equivariant map and similarly for
[X_{H}/H](S).
We define
\beta(P,\varphi)\coloneqq(P_{H},\varphi_{H})
to fit in the following diagram, where both the squares are cartesian:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces eH}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/H.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

It can be checked that
P_{H}
is a principal
H
-bundle over
S
and
\varphi_{H}
is
H
-equivariant, so that
\beta
actually defines a morphism

\beta:[X/G]\to[X_{H}/H].

The morphism
\beta
is an inverse to
\alpha
.
∎

We will apply Lemma
8.1.1
above in the case where
X=X_{\mathcal{C}}
,
G=\PGl_{2}
and
H\subseteq\PGl_{2}
is the maximal torus of diagonal matrices as follows. In the following, we identify
H\cong\mathbb{G}_{m}
, via the map
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\PGl_{2}
, which sends
z\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
to the class of
\begin{pmatrix}z&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
.

Recall that there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{C}_{1}\cong G/H
. Via this latter isomorphism, the projection on the first factor induces a
G
-equivariant morphism

p:X_{\mathcal{C}}\to G/H\cong\mathcal{C}_{1}

(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})\to X_{1}.

Notice that

(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}=\Biggl\{X_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{2},X_{3}\in\mathcal{C}_{3},X_{4}\in\mathcal{C}_{4}\ |\ X_{2}X_{3}X_{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{1}^{-1}&0\\
0&\lambda_{1}\end{pmatrix}\Biggr\}.

Denote by
(M_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}\coloneqq(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}/\!/H
. Lemma
8.1.1
implies that there is an isomorphism

(M_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}\cong M_{\mathcal{C}}.

We use similar notations for
(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H},(\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
. Reapplying Lemma
8.1.1
, we see that there is an isomorphism
(\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}\cong\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}}
. In particular,

H_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{s})_{H},q,t)=q^{2}t^{4}+3qt^{2}+t^{2}+t.

(8.1.11)

Consider now the character
\theta^{+}:H=\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
given by
\theta^{+}(z)=z
. The character
\theta
induces a linearization of the
H
-action on the affine variety
(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
(see for example
[
35
, Section 2]
). Using Mumford’s criterion (see
[
35
, Proposition 2.5]
), we check below that the semistable points
(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}^{ss,\theta^{+}}
are given by

(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}^{ss,\theta^{+}}=(Y^{+}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}.

In particular,
(Y^{+}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
is an open subset of
(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
and it is thus an algebraic variety. We denote by
(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}
the quotient stack
(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}=[Y_{\mathcal{C}}^{+}/H]
.

Since the action of
H
on
(Y^{+}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
is free, the stack
(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}
is an algebraic variety. Denote by
f^{+}:(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}\to(M_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
the canonical (proper) map.

We have indeed four type of points inside
(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
:

•

Notice that
O\cap(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
is the singleton
\{m\}
, corresponding to the quadruple (
8.1.2
). The point
m
, being a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
fixed point, is unstable. Indeed, considering the
1
-parameter subgroup
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
given by
\lambda(z)=z^{-1}
, we have
\langle\theta^{+},\lambda\rangle=-1<0
while it exists
\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0}\lambda(t)\cdot m=m
.

•

The points of
(X_{\mathcal{C}}^{s})_{H}
are stable. Each
x\in(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
corresponds to an irreducible representation. For a
1
-parameter subgroup
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the limit
\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0}\lambda(t)\cdot x
exists if and only if
\lambda
is trivial, i.e.
\langle\theta^{+},\lambda\rangle=0
.

•

The points of
(Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}
are semistable. Notice that
(Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}
is given by points of the form
m_{(a,b,c)}^{+}
as in eq.(
8.1.3
), for
(a,b,c)\in\mathbb{C}^{3}\setminus\{(0,0,0)\}
which respects eq.(
8.1.4
).

For
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
given by
\lambda(t)=t^{n}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
t\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
, we have

\lambda(t)\cdot m_{(a,b,c)}^{+}=m_{(t^{n}a,t^{n}b,t^{n}c)}.

In particular, we see that the limit
\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0}\lambda(t)\cdot m_{(a,b,c)}^{+}
exists (and it is given by
m
) if and only if
n\geq 0
, i.e. if and only if
\langle\theta^{+},\lambda\rangle\geq 0
.

•

By a similar reasoning, the points of
(Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{-})_{H}
are unstable.

We have that
(f^{+})^{-1}(m)=(Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}/H
. From the description of the elements of
X_{\mathcal{C}}
given at the beginning of
section

8.1
, we see that
(Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
. Via this identification
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\}
by scalar multiplication on both coordinates. We have therefore:

(Z_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}/H\cong(\mathbb{C}^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\})/\mathbb{G}_{m}=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}.

Consider now the Leray spectral sequence for compactly supported cohomology

E^{p,q}_{2}:H^{p}_{c}((M_{\mathcal{C}})_{H},R^{q}f^{+}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}})\Rightarrow H^{p+q}_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H},{\mathbb{Q}}).

Notice that
R^{q}f^{+}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}\neq 0
if and only if
q=0,2
. More precisely, we have
f^{+}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}={\mathbb{Q}}
and
R^{2}f^{+}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}=(i_{m})_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}
, where
i_{m}
is the closed embedding

i_{m}:\{m\}\to(M_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}.

Recall that the differential maps of the spectral sequence go in the direction

d_{r}^{p,q}:E^{p,q}_{r}\to E^{p+r,q-r+1}_{r}.

As
R^{q}f^{+}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}
is
0
for odd
q
, the differential
d_{2}^{p,q}
is the zero map for each
p,q
and therefore we have
E^{p,q}_{3}=E^{p,q}_{2}
for each
p,q
. Moreover, the differentials on the third page go in the direction
d^{p,q}_{3}:E^{p,q}_{3}\to E^{p+3,q-2}_{3}
and if
q\neq 0,2
, the vector space
E^{p,q}_{3}
is equal to
0
.

If
q=0
, we have
E^{3,-3}_{3}=\{0\}
and so
d^{p,q}_{3}=0
. Lastly, if
q=2
, we have
E^{p,q}_{3}=\{0\}
if
p\geq 1
and if
p=0
, we have
E^{3,0}_{3}=H^{3}_{c}((M_{\mathcal{C}})_{H},{\mathbb{Q}})=\{0\}
.

We deduce therefore that the differential maps
d^{p,q}_{3}
are all zero. In a similar way, it is possible to verify that
d^{p,q}_{r}=0
if
r\geq 2
, for any
p,q
and so that the spectral sequence collapses on the second page.

For each
n
, there is therefore an equality

H^{n}_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H},{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigoplus_{p+q=n}H^{p}_{c}((M_{\mathcal{C}})_{H},R^{q}f^{+}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}).

From the description of the sheaves
R^{q}f^{+}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}
given above, we deduce that we have

H_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H},q,t)=q^{2}t^{4}+4qt^{2}+t^{2}

(8.1.12)

A similar reasoning can be applied to the opposite linearization, induced by the character
\theta^{-}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
given by
\theta^{-}(z)=z^{-1}
. In this case, in a similar way we can argue that the semistable points
(X_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}^{ss,\theta^{-}}
are given by
(Y^{-}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
.

For the corresponding quotient
(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{-})_{H}
there is therefore an equality

H_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{-})_{H},q,t)=q^{2}t^{4}+4qt^{2}+t^{2}.

(8.1.13)

Denote now by
j^{+},j^{-}
the open embeddings
j^{+}:(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H}\to(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
and
j^{+}:(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{-})_{H}\to(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
and by
j
the open embedding
(\mathcal{N}^{s}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}\to(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}
. Notice that there is a short exact sequence of sheaves on
(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{!}\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{+}_{!}\mathbb{C}\oplus j_{!}^{-}\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

and therefore an associated long exact sequence in compactly supported cohomology
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i-1}_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{s})_{H})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{+})_{H})\oplus H^{i}_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}^{-})_{H})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{c}((\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H})}

From Lemma
8.1.1
, we deduce that there is an isomorphism
(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}})_{H}\cong\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}}
. From the long exact sequence above and eq.(
8.1.12
), eq.(
8.1.13
), eq.(
8.1.11
), it is therefore not difficult to show that

H_{c}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}},q,t)=q^{2}t^{4}+5qt^{2}+t^{2}+1.

(8.1.14)

Plugging this result into eq.(
8.1.8
) and using identity (
8.1.1
), we obtain finally identity (
8.0.4
).
